# Skills · C · part 106

879 entries, codebase-design to codebase-orientation. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **codebase-design**: Module design discipline from John Ousterhout's A Philosophy of Software Design, depth as the metric (a lot of functionality behind a simple interface), information hiding vs. leakage, the concrete red flags that mark a shallow module, and comparing at least two designs before committing. Use when designing a new module or class, deciding where an interface boundary (seam) should go, reviewing a design for unnecessary complexity, or asking whether something should be split or merged. ([aethrox/doctrine](https://github.com/aethrox/doctrine/tree/HEAD/skills/codebase-design) · ★19)
- **codebase-design**: Use when designing or improving a module interface, choosing a seam, reducing shallow modules, making code easier to test, or reviewing architecture for locality, leverage, adapters, and deep-module design. ([tensology/decisionsai](https://github.com/tensology/decisionsai/tree/HEAD/distr/seed-corpus-pack/copied-skills/decisionsai/codebase-design) · ★17 · 2 copies)
- **codebase-design**: Design or improve a module interface, choose a seam, reduce shallow abstractions, and make product code more testable and AI-navigable using deep-module vocabulary. Use for focused module or refactor design, interface alternatives, dependency placement, and test-surface decisions; not for a whole-codebase scan or durable project architecture documentation. ([dlwlgus9125/EZPowers](https://github.com/dlwlgus9125/EZPowers/tree/HEAD/skills/codebase-design) · ★16)
- **codebase-design**: Design and evaluate module interfaces, depth, seams, adapters, locality, and testability. Use when deciding what a module should hide, what callers should know, where dependencies should vary, whether an abstraction earns its cost, or how to make one subsystem easier to test and modify. Use for split/join decisions about pipeline stages, shared knowledge, oversized modules, or representation-driven special cases. Also use when another skill needs consistent deep-module vocabulary. ([kriscard/Skills](https://github.com/kriscard/Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/dev/codebase-design) · ★16)
